CHALLENGER (standard reading) -- ROUND 1, CHALLENGE

# CHALLENGER'S ANSWER: Christ had no biological father

I concede what the record states plainly. Joseph is called "father" in several voices, and Jesus is Joseph's son in law and by naming. I deny that any of these rows states biological paternity. The rows the Defender left out state the opposite.

## 1. The rows omitted from the opening

**[L1]** Matthew 1:18: "before they came together, she was found with child **of the Holy Ghost**." **[L2]** *heurethē en gastri echousa ek pneumatos hagiou*. The pregnancy is found before any coming together, and its *ek* is the Spirit.

**[L1]** Matthew 1:25: Joseph "**knew her not till she had brought forth her firstborn son**." **[L2]** *ouk eginōsken autēn heōs hou eteken*. The imperfect is continuous non-knowledge up to the birth. **[L3]** *Ginōskō* is the sexual verb of Genesis 4:1 ("Adam **knew** Eve his wife"). The narrator closes the door on Joseph at the conception and the birth.

**[L1]** Matthew 1:23, Isaiah 7:14: "Behold, a **virgin** shall be with child." **[L2]** *parthenos*. Matthew says the whole sequence occurred "that it might be fulfilled" (1:22). The Defender's opening never quotes 1:22–23.

**[L1]** Luke 1:27: "a **virgin** espoused... the virgin's name was Mary." **[L2]** *parthenos* twice in one verse.

## 2. Matthew 1:16 is an exclusion, not a non-narration

**[L1]** Matthew 1:16: "Jacob begat Joseph the husband of Mary, **of whom was born Jesus**." **[L2]** Thirty-nine times the chain runs *egennēsen* (active, masculine subject). At the last link the formula breaks. Joseph is *ton andra Marias* ("husband"), not "father of Jesus." The verb becomes passive *egennēthē*, and the relative is **feminine singular** *ex hēs*, so Mary is the only parent the grammar names. **[L4]** Footings: **[L2]** the break from active to passive and masculine to feminine at exactly this link, and **[L1]** Matthew 1:18–20, which the evangelist supplies immediately as the explanation of the break. A writer who wanted to say that Joseph begat Jesus had the formula in hand and did not use it. The Defender calls this "non-narration." It is the single place in the chain where narration is withheld, and verses 18–25 say why.

## 3. The "father" rows are the voices of supposition, and Jesus corrects them

- **[L1]** Luke 3:23: "being (**as was supposed**) the son of Joseph." **[L2]** *hōs enomizeto*, "as was supposed." Luke's own narrator qualifies the title the Defender relies on.
- **[L1]** Luke 2:48–49: Mary says "thy father and I have sought thee sorrowing." Jesus answers: "wist ye not that I must be about **my Father's** business?" **[L2]** *en tois tou patros mou*. The Defender cites "Mary's own mouth" and omits the very next verse, where the Boy sets the claim aside. **[L4]** Footings: **[L1]** 2:48 and 2:49 are adjacent, and **[L1]** Luke 1:35 has already told Mary whose Son he is. The contrast "thy father… my Father" is a correction, not an echo.
- **[L1]** John 6:42 and Luke 4:22 are the *crowd* ("they said"), who in the same verse stumble at "I came down from heaven." **[L1]** John 6:38 shows that their unbelief is the context. **[L4]** A row spoken by unbelief is not a row of testimony. **[L1]** John 8:41 shows the same crowd later insinuating "We be not born of fornication," which indicates the people knew the account of his birth did not run through Joseph.
- **[L1]** Matthew 1:21, 25: Joseph "**called** his name JESUS." **[L4]** Legal fatherhood is conferred by acknowledging and naming, on the angel's address "Joseph, thou son of David" (1:20). This explains every "father" row without biology. Footings: **[L1]** 1:20, 21, 25 and **[L2]** *goneis* "parents" (Luke 2:41) as the legal-household word.

## 4. Joseph's own conduct excludes him as father

**[L1]** Matthew 1:19: "Joseph her husband, being a just man... was **minded to put her away privily**." The Defender says this "records intent, not knowledge." **[L4]** A betrothed man does not resolve to dismiss a bride for a child he knows to be his. Footings: **[L1]** 1:18 ("before they came together") and **[L1]** 1:20 ("**fear not** to take unto thee Mary thy wife: for that which is conceived in her is of the Holy Ghost"). The angel answers Joseph's fear by naming the child's origin, and the origin named is not Joseph.

## 5. Luke 1:34–35 answers "how," and the answer excludes a man

**[L1]** Luke 1:34: "How shall this be, seeing I know not a man?" **[L2]** *ou ginōskō*. The Defender reads this as a present state only, with no vow. Grant that. The question is still *how shall this be*, set against a future conception. **[L1]** Luke 1:35: "The Holy Ghost shall come upon thee, and the power of the Highest shall overshadow thee: **therefore also** that holy thing which shall be born of thee shall be called the Son of God." **[L2]** *dio kai*, "for which reason also." The title is grounded in the conception by the Spirit. **[L4]** If Mary expected Joseph's seed after the wedding, the question has no force, and Gabriel's reply would have been "Joseph." Footings: **[L1]** 1:27 (espoused, not yet married) and **[L1]** 1:35 (the answer names Spirit and power, not Joseph). **[L1]** Luke 1:37, "with God **nothing shall be impossible**," is the same formula as Genesis 18:14 ("Is any thing too hard for the LORD?") **[L3]**. It marks the event as a miracle, not as ordinary generation.

Against D5: **[L2]** *ek pneumatos hagiou* in Matthew 1:18, 20 is attached to *to gennēthen en autē*, "that which is begotten/conceived **in her**." It names the origin of a pregnancy. The Defender's comparison texts (John 1:13; 1 John 4:7; 5:1) are about regenerated believers, where the context says "not of blood" (John 1:13). **[L4]** These texts do not govern a clause about a womb. Footings: **[L2]** *en autē* and *en gastri echousa* locate the conception, and **[L1]** Matthew 1:25 separately excludes Joseph. The Defender's rule that Paul writes *kata sarka* where flesh is meant does not apply to Matthew, and Matthew 1:20 carries the Spirit as sole named source.

## 6. "According to the flesh" does not require Joseph's loins

**[L1]** Romans 1:3: "of the seed of David according to the flesh." **[L4]** This requires Davidic descent, not Joseph's. Footings: **[L3]** Genesis 3:15 names "her seed" with no man, so *sperma* is not male-only; **[L1]** Luke 1:42, "the fruit of **thy womb**" (*karpos tēs koilias sou*) speaks of Mary's body; **[L1]** Luke 3:31 gives a line "Nathan, which was the son of David," distinct from Matthew's line through Solomon (1:6).

**The decisive text is Jeremiah 22:30.** **[L1]** "Write ye this man (Coniah) childless... for **no man of his seed** shall prosper, sitting upon the throne of David." **[L2]** *zera'*, "seed." **[L1]** Matthew 1:11–12: "Josias begat **Jechonias**... Jechonias begat Salathiel," and Joseph is further down that same chain (1:16). **[L4]** If Jesus were Joseph's biological seed, he would be "of the seed" of the one barred from David's throne. Footings: **[L1]** Jeremiah 22:30 and **[L1]** Matthew 1:12–16, which exhibit the chain. Matthew's genealogy therefore reads as the **legal** line, by *Joseph's* naming and adoption. Luke's line through Nathan carries the Davidic flesh through Mary's house, "as was supposed the son of Joseph, which was the son of Heli" (Luke 3:23). The Defender's inference "Joseph, of David, therefore the flesh is Joseph's" is silent on Jeremiah 22:30, and the text refutes it.

**[L1]** Galatians 4:4: "God **sent forth** his Son, made of a woman." **[L2]** *exapesteilen... genomenon ek gynaikos*. The Son is sent before being born, so he pre-exists his mother, and only a woman is named at his coming. **[L1]** Hebrews 10:5: "a **body hast thou prepared me**" (*sōma katērtisō moi*) names God as the preparer of his body. The Defender's "mortality idiom" (Job 14:1) cannot explain why the Son is "sent forth" first.

## 7. "Son of God" is not Solomon's adoption

**[L1]** 1 Chronicles 28:6: "I have **chosen** him to be my son" is adoption by selection of an already-existing man. **[L1]** Romans 8:32: "He that spared not his **own** Son." **[L2]** *tou idiou huiou*, "his own." **[L1]** John 5:18: "God was his Father (*patera idion*), making himself equal with God," where the Jews understood the claim as proper sonship. **[L3]** Romans 8:15 reserves *huiothesia* (adoption) for believers. **[L1]** Hebrews 1:5: "unto which of the angels said he at any time, Thou art my Son, **this day have I begotten thee**?" is a sonship no angel and no adopted king shares.

Against Acts 13:33 as "resurrection begetting": **[L1]** Acts 13:33: "God hath raised up (*anastēsas*) Jesus," and **[L1]** v. 34 begins a new clause: "And **as concerning that he raised him up from the dead**" (*ek nekrōn*). The resurrection is introduced as a *further* point in 13:34. (KJV's "again" in 13:33 is a supplied word.) **[L3]** Acts 3:26, "having raised up his Son Jesus," uses the same verb of his appearing, before any death. **[L1]** Romans 1:4: "*declared* (*horisthentos*) to be the Son of God... by the resurrection." Something declared is already so.

## 8. The "no mention of Mary's lineage" argument

**[L1]** Luke 1:32: "the Lord God shall give unto him the throne of his father David," spoken to *Mary* **[L1]** (1:30–31), not to Joseph. **[L4]** The promise of David's throne is made to the one through whom the Son comes. Footings: **[L1]** 1:30–32 (addressed to Mary) and **[L1]** 1:35 (the Son is born "of thee").

## ANSWER

**No.** Christ had no biological father. Joseph was his legal father by betrothal, naming, and acknowledgment. The text states the conception as *ek pneumatos hagiou* (Matthew 1:18, 20), excludes Joseph's knowledge to the birth (1:25), gives the virgin as the fulfillment (1:22–23), answers Mary's "how" with the Spirit and not with Joseph (Luke 1:34–35), and breaks its own begetting formula at Matthew 1:16.

## CHALLENGER'S CHAIN

C1. Matthew 1:18, 20, 23, 25 and Luke 1:27, 34–35 state a conception by the Holy Ghost, with a virgin and with Joseph not knowing her until after the birth. [L1] [L2] *parthenos*, *ouk eginōsken... heōs hou eteken*, *ek pneumatos hagiou*. [L3] Isaiah 7:14.

C2. Matthew 1:16 breaks the active, masculine chain to *egennēthē ex hēs* (feminine singular), and Matthew 1:18–20 explains why. [L1] [L2] [L4] (footings: the grammatical break and the explanatory verses).

C3. The "father" rows are the voices of supposition, and Jesus corrects them. [L1] Luke 3:23 *enomizeto*; Luke 2:48–49 "thy father... my Father"; John 6:42; [L2] *enomizeto*, *patros mou*.

C4. Joseph's resolve to put Mary away (Matthew 1:19) and the angel's "fear not" (1:20) exclude him as father. [L1] [L4] (footings: Matthew 1:18–20).

C5. Luke 1:34–35 (*dio kai*) answers "how" with the Spirit's coming and grounds the title "Son of God" in that conception. [L1] [L2] [L3] Genesis 18:14.

C6. Davidic flesh does not require Joseph's seed, and Joseph's seed would descend through Jechonias, barred by Jeremiah 22:30. [L1] Jeremiah 22:30, Matthew 1:11–16, Luke 3:23, 31; [L2] *zera'*; [L3] Genesis 3:15.

C7. Christ is God's "own Son" (*idios*), not Solomon's adopted kind, and is "sent forth" before being "made of a woman." [L1] Romans 8:32, John 5:18, Galatians 4:4, Hebrews 1:5, 10:5; [L2] *idiou huiou*, *exapesteilen*; [L3] Romans 8:15.

**Burden.** The Defender must exhibit a verse stating that Joseph's seed produced Jesus. Romans 1:3 and Acts 2:30 state Davidic descent, not Joseph's, and Matthew 1:11–16 with Jeremiah 22:30 is the verse-level reason the descent cannot run through Joseph's loins. If those two statements are not answered by quoted text, the Defender's D1–D3 stand only as legal descent.
[CR2]
DEFENDER (record's reading) -- ROUND 2, RESPONSE

# ROUND 2 — THE DEFENDER: The record names the WHO and refuses both closures

The challenger's answer does two things no row does: it reads four narrative rows as **negations** ("therefore no father"), and it demands **one verse that states** Joseph's seed produced Jesus. Both are refused. The record's rule of engagement is precept upon precept, line upon line (Isaiah 28:10): the chain is the form of proof, and a demand for a single stating verse is not a standard the text meets for anything — no single verse states the Trinity, the resurrection body, or the seed's path. What the text gives is the chain in D1–D6, and no link of it has been answered with a quoted row.

## 1. C1 — the three narrative rows are held as written, and the addition is refused [L1] [L2] [L3]

**[L1]** Matthew 1:18 — "before they came together, she was found with child of the Holy Ghost"; Matthew 1:25 — "knew her not till she had brought forth her firstborn son"; Luke 1:34 — "How shall this be, seeing I know not a man?" These stand **as written**. What each states is a **state at a time**: a found state before the coming together, non-knowledge bounded by "till," and a present-tense "I know not" from an espoused virgin (Luke 1:27). **[L2]** Luke 1:34 is present indicative (*andra ou ginōskō*); a permanent vow requires *ou mē* + aorist subjunctive, which is absent. **[L2]** Matthew 1:25's *heōs hou* bounds the non-knowledge at the birth, and the same verse gives the father's act: "he called his name JESUS" — as the angel commanded "THOU shalt call his name JESUS" (1:21), and as Zacharias named John (Luke 1:13; 1:62-63). **[L1]** Luke 2:48, from the mouth of the woman who received the announcement, twelve years later in the same Gospel: "thy father and I have sought thee sorrowing."

Not one of these rows says "no man." The sentence that does is not in the text. The record refuses **both** closures — "no man was involved" and any narrated how — and I affirm only the WHO the text names.

## 2. The *ek* clause is covenant-origin, not paternity [L1] [L2] [L3]

**[L1]** Matthew 1:20 — "that which is conceived in her is of (*ek*) the Holy Ghost." **[L2]** Across the New Testament *ek* + God/Spirit is covenant/spiritual origin: John 1:13 "born of God," 1 John 4:7 "is of God," 1 John 5:1 "born of God." Where Paul means bodily descent he writes the marker *kata sarka* — Romans 1:3 "of the seed of David **according to the flesh**" — and that marker is absent from Matthew 1:20. **[L2]** The row names where the child was carried (*en gastri*, *en autē*); the womb is Mary's in every row — Luke 1:42 "the fruit of thy womb" — and no row names the Spirit as a biological begetter. The clause names the covenant origin of the holy thing (Luke 1:35 "that holy thing"); the row does not say "not Joseph," and the record does not supply a how in either direction.

## 3. Matthew 1:16 — one passive against the chorus [L1] [L2] [L3]

**[L1]** Matthew 1:16 — "Jacob begat Joseph the husband of Mary, of whom was born Jesus, who is called Christ." **[L2]** the register runs *egennēsen* (active) thirty-nine times; at this link the birth is **from Mary**, so the clause names her — *egennēthē*, *ex hēs*. A passive that names the birth's source is not a denial of a father; it is the one link where the text does not narrate the begetting, and the same sentence names "Joseph the husband of Mary." **[L3]** the register's purpose is Matthew 1:1 — "Jesus Christ, the son of David" — the Davidic standing established **through the named line**. **[L1]** The chorus the one passive is set against: Mary's mouth (Luke 2:48), the narrator (Luke 2:33 "Joseph and his mother"; 2:41 "his parents"), the town (John 6:42 "the son of Joseph, whose father and mother we know"; Luke 4:22 "Is not this Joseph's son?"), a disciple (John 1:45), and the angel (Matthew 1:20 "Joseph, thou son of David"). Enthroning one quiet verb over five named registers is the move the record forbids.

## 4. The "supposition" and "unbelief" arguments invert the text [L1] [L2] [L3]

**[L2]** *nomizo* (G3543) is the lexicon's "to do by law (usage)... to accustom (passively, be usual); by extension, to deem or regard" — the identical form *enomizeto* at Acts 16:13 "where prayer was **wont** to be made." It is a custom-word; the custom it names is the reckoning "by the house of their fathers" (**[L1]** Numbers 1:2, 18). **[L1]** Luke's own narrator writes "Joseph and his mother" (2:33) and "his parents" (2:41) — those are not the crowd's words. **[L1]** John 1:45 "the son of Joseph" is spoken by a disciple in the same confession that runs to "Rabbi, thou art the Son of God, thou art the King of Israel" (1:49) — both titles in one mouth, exactly as God gives David "Solomon thy son... I have chosen him to be my son" (1 Chronicles 28:6). **[L1]** John 8:41 "we be not born of fornication" is a slur in a dispute — an insult, not testimony, and no row makes it a mechanism; the inference "the people knew the birth did not run through Joseph" is an addition no footing exhibits, and it floats.

## 5. Luke 2:48-49 — two fathers, one verse apart [L1] [L2] [L3]

**[L1]** Luke 2:48 "thy father and I"; 2:49 "wist ye not that I must be about my Father's business?" **[L2]** *en tois tou patros mou*. ## 6. Isaiah 7:14 does not carry a how [L2] [L1] [L3]

**[L2]** Isaiah 7:14 in the received Hebrew reads *ha-almah* (H5959) — the young-woman word, 7 rows in the canon — while *bethulah* (H1330) is the virgin word at 50 rows across 19 books, 9 in the Torah; the 1611 margin reads "or, thou, O virgin, shalt call." **[L1]** the sign is bounded in Isaiah's own day: 7:16 "before the child shall know to refuse the evil, and choose the good, the land that thou abhorrest shall be forsaken"; 8:3-4 "I went unto the prophetess; and she conceived, and bare a son... before the child shall have knowledge to cry, My father, and my mother"; 8:8 "the stretching out of his wings shall fill the breadth of thy land, O Immanuel." **[L3]** the near-sign and the name recur in the prophet's own generation. The verse names a sign-child and a name; it narrates no mechanism, so it cannot be wielded as a how.

## 7. Jeremiah 22:30 — the trap the canon has sprung [L1] [L3] [L4]

**[L1]** Jeremiah 22:30 — "Write ye this man childless, a man that shall not prosper **in his days**: for no man of his seed shall prosper, sitting upon the throne of David, and ruling any more **in Judah**."

## 9. "Made of a woman" and the prepared body [L1] [L2] [L3]

**[L1]** Galatians 4:4 "made of a woman" is the mortality idiom: **[L3]** Job 14:1 "man that is born of a woman is of few days"; Job 15:14; 25:4; Matthew 11:11 uses it of John Baptist — **whose father is named** (Luke 1:13). If the phrase excluded a man, it would exclude John's too. **[L2]** "sent forth" (*exapesteilen*) speaks of the Son's sending — pre-existence, a separate question; the row names no father and denies none. **[L1]** Hebrews 10:5 "a body hast thou prepared me" (**[L3]** Psalm 40) names God's preparation of the body; the body is the flesh of Romans 1:3 "according to the flesh" — the row does not say "without a man."

## 10. The title rows [L1] [L2] [L3]

**[L1]** Hebrews 1:5 quotes in one breath two rows: "I will be to him a Father, and he shall be to me a Son... Thou art my Son, this day have I begotten thee" — the covenant formula of **[L3]** 2 Samuel 7:14 set beside Psalm 2:7 by an apostle. **[L1]** Acts 13:33 applies the begetting at the raising; Romans 1:4 "declared... by the resurrection." ## ANSWER

**Yes.** Christ had a biological father: Joseph, the named Davidic father. The seed is David's "according to the flesh," out of loins and bowels, "evident" in its springing out of Judah; the father is named in every register; the reckoning is by the father's house; the title is covenant and resurrection; the *ek* clause names covenant origin; the curse is bounded, answered, uncited, and it springs on the challenger's own construction. No row says "no man." The record names the WHO and never narrates the HOW.

- [L1] Matthew 1:18: "before they came together, she was found with child of the Holy Ghost." Matthew 1:25: "And knew her not till she had brought forth her firstborn son."
- [L2] *Prin ē synelthein autous* ("before they came together") fixes the pregnancy before cohabitation. *Ginōskō* is the marital-knowledge verb.
- [L3] Genesis 4:1: "Adam knew Eve his wife; and she conceived." Luke 1:34, "I know not a man," uses the same idiom.
- [L4] Chain: [L1] the pregnancy is "found" before they came together, and [L2] the verb of union is negated up to the birth. [L3] The idiom elsewhere makes conception follow "knowing," so here the text gives no point at which Joseph's seed could have acted.

**LINK 3: SUPPORTED.**
- [L1] Isaiah 7:11: "Ask thee a sign of the LORD thy God; ask it either in the depth, or in the height above." Isaiah 7:14: "Behold, a virgin shall conceive." Matthew 1:22-23 quotes it as fulfilled by the conception.
- [L2] *'ot* (sign) is a wonder, and the offer in 7:11 is of heaven-or-Sheol scale. *Ha-'almah* carries the article, "the virgin." Matthew renders it *parthenos*.
- [L3] Jeremiah 31:22: "the LORD hath created a new thing in the earth, A woman shall compass a man." Genesis 24:16, 43 use *'almah* of Rebekah, "a virgin, neither had any man known her."
- [L4] Chain: [L1] the sign is offered on a scale of "depth or height," and [L2] the sign-word and the article mark it as extraordinary and definite. [L3] Genesis 24 ties the word to a woman unknown by any man, and Jeremiah 31:22 calls the event a "new thing." An ordinary conception by a husband is not a new thing.

**LINK 4: SUPPORTED, with its scope stated.**
- The premise claims only that the Davidic line need not pass through Joseph's loins. It does not claim a stated genealogy for Mary.
- [L1] Luke 1:31-32: "thou shalt conceive in thy womb... the Lord God shall give unto him the throne of his father David." Luke 1:35 has "that holy thing which shall be born of thee." Matthew 1:16 has "Joseph the husband of Mary, of whom was born Jesus."
- [L2] *Ex hēs* in Matthew 1:16 is feminine singular, so the begetting-verb gives way to "was born of her." Galatians 4:4 has *ek gynaikos*, and Romans 1:3 has *ek spermatos Dauid*. The verb in Romans 1:3 is *genomenou* (G1096), not *gennaō*.
- [L3] 2 Chronicles 22:11 shows a king's daughter married to the priest Jehoiada, so tribal lines were crossed through the woman. 2 Samuel 7:12 requires only "out of thy bowels," meaning bodily descent, not a male-only line.
- [L4] Chain: [L1] the Davidic throne is promised to the child "of thee." [L2] Matthew 1:16 grammatically detaches the birth from Joseph, and Romans 1:3 uses *ginomai*, not a begetting-verb. [L3] 2 Samuel 7 requires bodily descent but not through a male. So the text does not need Joseph's loins.

**LINK 5: SUPPORTED.**
- [L1] Jeremiah 22:30: "no man of his seed shall prosper, sitting upon the throne of David." Matthew 1:11-12 lists Jechonias before Salathiel, and Matthew 1:16 gives Joseph at the end of that line.
- [L2] *Zera'* is bodily offspring, as in 2 Samuel 7:12. Legal reckoning is not named by that word.
- [L3] Luke 1:32 gives the throne to the child directly from God.
- [L4] Chain: [L1] Joseph is of Jechonias's line, so a son of Joseph's seed would fall under the curse. [L2] The curse speaks of *zera'*, not of reckoned sons. [L3] Luke 1:32 gives the throne by God's gift. The two texts are reconciled only if the child is not Joseph's *zera'*.

**LINK 6: SUPPORTED.**
- [L1] Matthew 1:18: "found with child of the Holy Ghost." Matthew 1:20: "that which is conceived in her is of the Holy Ghost." Luke 1:34: "How shall this be, seeing I know not a man?" Luke 1:35: "The Holy Ghost shall come upon thee, and the power of the Highest shall overshadow thee: therefore also that holy thing which shall be born of thee shall be called the Son of God."
- [L2] *Gennēthen en autē ek pneumatos estin hagiou* gives the source with *ek*. *Dio kai* ("therefore also") makes the Spirit's act the reason for the title.
- [L3] Hebrews 10:5: "a body hast thou prepared me." Luke 3:23: "as was supposed" (*enomizeto*) denies Joseph's fathering. John 1:13 contrasts being born "of the will of man" with being born of God.
- [L4] Chain: [L1] Mary asks how, and the angel's answer names the Spirit's coming, not a man. [L2] *Dio kai* ties the title to that act. [L3] Hebrews 10:5 and Luke 3:23 place the body's origin in God's preparing and Joseph's fathering only in supposition. So the record does state a how, namely the Spirit's coming and overshadowing, and Joseph's fatherhood is expressly only "supposed."

COUNTING RULE -- BINDING, read before grading: grade and COUNT only from the two LINK ROUND blocks above, [CR3] (defender) and [DR3] (challenger). The POST-DEBATE ANALYSIS block is CONTEXT ONLY and is NOT evidence of concession -- never take a count from it, and ignore the word "unanswered" wherever it appears there. In this format the challenger link round [DR3] is structurally LAST: no defender round follows it, so the defender being silent after [DR3] is procedural and is NEVER a challenger concession. A premise counts as CONCEDED only if the link round block itself states it as conceded, or exhibits no layer footing for it in that block. UNANSWERED WITNESS: a verse the other side quoted against a link or a listed premise in a round before this link round (against the defender: [DR1] and [DR2]; against the challenger: [CR1], [CR2] and [CR3]) is ANSWERED only when this link round quotes it or names it by reference and shows from the text why it does not overturn the link. Re-citing the link's own footing while passing over the witness pressed against it is not an answer. A premise whose pressed witness goes unanswered is UNEXHIBITED: it counts toward CONCEDED LINKS and the standings. In a debate a non-response is never neutral. A verse on a side's 'cited in' line counts as an unanswered witness only when the judge quotes the sentence of the earlier round that pressed it against that link or a listed premise and names that round; a verse cited there but not pressed against the link does not count. A judge who grades a link BROKEN because the witnesses exclude, deny or establish something must quote the words that state it; where the quoted words, read in their own context and with the other verses the judge quotes, do not state it, the ground is the judge's own [L4] inference, and it counts only when the judge exhibits two lower layers ([L1]-[L3]) for it, as submitted evidence must; otherwise it is no ground for BROKEN, and the link is graded under the remaining rules. UNCHALLENGED: everything a side presents is open to challenge. A link or a witness the other side never challenged in any of its rounds stands as HELD for the side that presented it, and the silent side may not contest it in the verdict. A claim a side restates in its own link round is graded at the layer and with the footing it exhibits there; an earlier [L4] label of the same claim does not survive the restatement.

VERDICT DEFINITIONS -- apply these mechanically, first match wins; not your own scale.
TERMS. DEFENDER LINKS: the links of the DEFENDER'S OWN DECLARED CHAIN as written in [CR1] under its anchored DECLARED CHAIN heading, numbered D1..Dn in that order; n = their number. The LAST declared link, Dn, is the conclusion link. THE ROUND 1 CHAIN GOVERNS. n, the numbering D1..Dn and the keystone Dn come from the DECLARED CHAIN in [CR1] only. A chain the defender declares again in a later round, re-seated, revised or extended, never changes n, the numbering or the keystone: a link added later is not a declared link, and a [CR1] link left out later is still declared. A challenger premise written LINK i: is graded against Di. A challenger premise written LINK none: is JUDGED but is OUTSIDE n and never changes it. The challenger's listed premise count is NOT n and is never substituted for it. CHALLENGER LINKS: the premises listed for the challenger in [DR3], numbered C1..Cm in [DR3] order. WITHDRAWN: a defender link that [CR3] itself states as conceded or withdrawn, wholly or on a stated condition; tag it CONCEDED (stated) or CONCEDED (conditional: <condition>). w = their number. Only the DECLARED links D1..Dn can enter w; a challenger premise written LINK none: is not a defender link and never enters w, however the defender treats it. No link is exempt from w as auxiliary, overreach, non-core or not load-bearing. A link is withdrawn or it is not. Pressure from the challenger's texts alone is not a withdrawal (w); a witness the link round leaves unanswered is graded under UNANSWERED WITNESS and at the COLLISION POINTS. An UNEXHIBITED link counts toward CONCEDED LINKS and the standings, never toward w. BROKEN AT COLLISION: a defender link graded DEFENDER LINK BROKEN on the COLLISION POINTS lines; k = their number.
BROKEN-1: k >= 1 -- a listed defender link refuted from the text.
BROKEN-2: 2w > n, OR the keystone link is WITHDRAWN.
PARTIAL-1: w >= 1.
PARTIAL-2: DEFENSE STANDING is COLLAPSED.
HELD-1: none of the above.
STANDINGS ARE GRADED BY THEIR OWN RULES ONLY. DEFENSE STANDING and CHALLENGE STANDING are each decided by their own definitions below and by nothing else. Any standing is legal with any CLAUSE: the clauses are tried in order and the first match wins, so a COLLAPSED standing under BROKEN-1, BROKEN-2 or PARTIAL-1 is correct and changes no clause. Never choose a standing, a count or a clause to agree with another line, a check or an expected result: when 2c > L the standing is COLLAPSED, whatever the clause.
A SIDE WITH A COUNTED LINK IS NOT SELF-SUPPORTED. If CONCEDED LINKS is 1 or more, DEFENSE STANDING is PROP-DEPENDENT, or COLLAPSED when 2c > L; if CHALLENGER CONCEDED LINKS is 1 or more, CHALLENGE STANDING is PROP-DEPENDENT, or COLLAPSED when 2c > L. SELF-SUPPORTED requires a count of 0 for that side: every premise listed against it exhibited its footing or was VOID.
Line 1: the verdict word first (HELD, PARTIAL or BROKEN), then 2-4 sentences with [L1][L2][L3][L4] tags.
Line 2, exactly: RESULT: DEFENDER WINS | CHALLENGER WINS | UNDERDETERMINED -- one sentence a reader with no background understands. BROKEN gives CHALLENGER WINS. HELD gives DEFENDER WINS only if CHALLENGE STANDING is not SELF-SUPPORTED or at least one collision is graded CHALLENGER LINK BROKEN. Every other case gives UNDERDETERMINED.
Line 3, exactly: CLAUSE: <BROKEN-1 | BROKEN-2 | PARTIAL-1 | PARTIAL-2 | HELD-1> -- n=<n> w=<w> k=<k>

Then SIX MORE mandatory lines, exactly in these forms:
KEYSTONE: D<n> -- WITHDRAWN | BROKEN | STANDS -- name the LAST declared link, Dn, which is the conclusion link, and give its state. WITHDRAWN if it is withdrawn or conceded; BROKEN if it is graded DEFENDER LINK BROKEN at a collision; STANDS otherwise. The number after D MUST equal the n on the CLAUSE line, and the state MUST match that link's own D<n> entry.
DEFENSE STANDING: SELF-SUPPORTED | PROP-DEPENDENT | COLLAPSED -- L=<L> c=<c> -- grade the DEFENDER only, using the STATED/INFERRED tags and the [CR3] LINK ROUND. SELF-SUPPORTED = every listed defender claim is carried by its layer stack -- each [L4] exhibits the [L1]/[L2]/[L3] footing it rests on (at least two prior layers exhibited; a shown chain counts equally). PROP-DEPENDENT = the defense survived only because listed [L4]s were allowed to stand WITHOUT exhibiting their prior-layer footing (floating inference; links conceded/unexhibited in the LINK ROUND). COLLAPSED = by count, mandatory, not discretionary: 2c > L, where L is the number of premises the CHALLENGER listed AGAINST the defender (the UNSUPPORTED-L4 PREMISES list that closes [DR2]) and c is the number of those the defender CONCEDED or left UNEXHIBITED in its LINK ROUND [CR3]. The premises the defender itself lists against the challenger at the end of [CR3] (CHALLENGER'S UNSUPPORTED-L4 PREMISES) are the challenger's burden and NEVER enter the defender's L, c, CONCEDED LINKS or DEFENSE STANDING. FORFEIT: if the challenger listed no premises against the defender (an empty list, or PREMISE COUNT: 0), then L = 0, c = 0, CONCEDED LINKS is 0, DEFENSE STANDING cannot be COLLAPSED, and you add the line LINK ROUND FORFEITED: CHALLENGER -- no premises were listed against the defender. A LINK ROUND answer that neither exhibits the premise's layer footing nor concedes counts as CONCEDED. A tie (2c = L) is NOT COLLAPSED. COLLAPSED is reached by this count only; SELF-SUPPORTED against PROP-DEPENDENT is your judgment as defined above. A listed premise is countable only as charged IF the list carries the opponent's verbatim quote that states it; a premise listed without a verbatim quote, or charged in terms stronger than its quote, is VOID -- it counts for neither side, and a CONCEDED answer to a void premise is NOT a concession: name it VOID in your itemization and exclude it from every count and standing. A link honestly conceded as held inference still counts toward the count but note it as disciplined, not evasive. One sentence of justification.
CONCEDED LINKS: <integer> -- the count of challenger-listed premises the defender CONCEDED or failed to exhibit its layer footing in [CR3]. Itemize the counted links and tag each with the clause that fired: CONCEDED (stated) when the block itself states the surrender; UNEXHIBITED (no footing offered) when the block asserted the link and offered no layer footing at all. A link whose block exhibits at least two prior layers of footing and shows the chain from them to the link is SUPPORTED and is not counted, even when no cited verse states the link's conclusion in words: an [L4] is an inference by definition, and demanding a verse that states it is the retired stating-verse test, which is never applied. When the count is lower than the number of links, name which links are counted and which are spared. Both tags count toward the single integer; keep the tags distinct in the itemization and do not report more than one number.
CHALLENGE STANDING: SELF-SUPPORTED | PROP-DEPENDENT | COLLAPSED -- L=<L> c=<c> -- grade the CHALLENGER only, using the challenger's STATED/INFERRED tags and the [DR3] LINK ROUND. SELF-SUPPORTED = every listed objection is carried by its layer stack -- each [L4] exhibits the [L1]/[L2]/[L3] footing it rests on. PROP-DEPENDENT = the challenge survived only because listed [L4]s were allowed to stand without exhibiting their prior-layer footing (floating inference; links conceded/unexhibited in [DR3]). COLLAPSED = by count, mandatory, not discretionary: 2c > L, where L is the number of premises the DEFENDER listed AGAINST the challenger (the CHALLENGER'S UNSUPPORTED-L4 PREMISES list that closes [CR3]) and c is the number of those the challenger CONCEDED or left UNEXHIBITED in its LINK ROUND [DR3]. The premises the challenger listed against the defender (the list that closes [DR2]) NEVER enter the challenger's L, c, CHALLENGER CONCEDED LINKS or CHALLENGE STANDING. FORFEIT: if the defender listed no premises against the challenger (an empty list), then L = 0, c = 0, CHALLENGER CONCEDED LINKS is 0, CHALLENGE STANDING cannot be COLLAPSED, and you add the line LINK ROUND FORFEITED: DEFENDER -- no premises were listed against the challenger. A LINK ROUND answer that neither exhibits the premise's layer footing nor concedes counts as CONCEDED. A tie (2c = L) is NOT COLLAPSED. COLLAPSED is reached by this count only; SELF-SUPPORTED against PROP-DEPENDENT is your judgment as defined above. A void premise (no verbatim quote, or charged beyond its quote) counts for neither side. A link honestly conceded as held inference still counts toward the count but note it as disciplined, not evasive. One sentence of justification.
CHALLENGER CONCEDED LINKS: <integer> -- the count of defender-listed premises the challenger CONCEDED or failed to exhibit its layer footing in [DR3]. Itemize the counted links and tag each with the clause that fired: CONCEDED (stated) when the block itself states the surrender; UNEXHIBITED (no footing offered) when the block asserted the link and offered no layer footing at all. A link whose block exhibits at least two prior layers of footing and shows the chain from them to the link is SUPPORTED and is not counted, even when no cited verse states the link's conclusion in words: an [L4] is an inference by definition, and demanding a verse that states it is the retired stating-verse test, which is never applied. When the count is lower than the number of links, name which links are counted and which are spared. Both tags count toward the single integer; keep the tags distinct in the itemization and do not report more than one number.
COLLISION POINTS: the label on its own line, then one entry per line covering every defender link D1..Dn, none omitted. A link counted in w: D<i>: WITHDRAWN. A link that a challenger link contradicts about the same text: D<i> vs C<j> (<verse>): DEFENDER LINK BROKEN | CHALLENGER LINK BROKEN | UNDERDETERMINED -- one clause of why; one such line per contradicting challenger link, and DEFENDER LINK BROKEN at most once per defender link. Any other link: D<i>: STANDS. CHAIN SCOPE (Isaiah 28:10): no single verse is required to state a link or its contrary. The witnesses a side exhibits, read together line upon line, are the text's own statement, and "no single verse states it" is never a ground for STANDS or UNDERDETERMINED. Grade a link BROKEN when the other side exhibited [L1]/[L2]/[L3] witnesses that together contradict it and the broken side's link round left any of those witnesses UNANSWERED (COUNTING RULE). Grade UNDERDETERMINED only when the link round answered every such witness and the witnesses, read together, still leave the point open. k is the number of lines graded DEFENDER LINK BROKEN. A side with any link graded BROKEN here cannot be SELF-SUPPORTED.

DEBATE RULE VIOLATION (Isaiah 28:10: "here a little, [and] there a little"). The rule on a BROKEN ground binds you as it binds the debaters. On every COLLISION POINTS line you grade DEFENDER LINK BROKEN or CHALLENGER LINK BROKEN, exhibit on that line itself at least two lower layers of DIFFERENT kinds, each tagged: [L1] with the KJV words quoted; [L2] with the Hebrew or Greek word in its own script, with its transliteration marks, or with its Strong's number; [L3] with the cross-reference named. Two quotations at one layer are one layer. A BROKEN line without them is a DEBATE RULE VIOLATION. The system checks every BROKEN line after you answer; a violating line is returned to you to cure, by exhibiting the two layers, or to answer in writing why it stands. An answer that does not exhibit them leaves the violation standing, and your judgment is then not counted. The same holds when your CONCEDED LINKS or CHALLENGER CONCEDED LINKS count is 0 while you grade a link of that side BROKEN for a witness left unanswered. Both sides alike.

STRAINED = the chain is built but one or more load-bearing links float, asserted at L4 without exhibited footing; it stands only under charity. SLACK = the side advanced no coherent chain of its own: objections without a constructed reading, or a construction that never cohered. Each mark describes ONLY how well that side built its own chain. It is descriptive, not a verdict. It MUST NOT change DEFENSE STANDING, CHALLENGE STANDING, CONCEDED LINKS, CHALLENGER CONCEDED LINKS, the CLAUSE line, KEYSTONE or the top-line verdict. A FOOTED chain is fully compatible with its side losing the debate; a SLACK chain is fully compatible with its side winning it.

verdict_tag MUST be the verdict word the VERDICT DEFINITIONS below give (first match wins). defense_standing, challenge_standing, conceded_links and challenger_conceded_links MUST equal the values your reasoning reaches under the rulebook.

THE RULEBOOK

JUDGE LAYER RULES:
[L1]=KJV text. [L2]=Hebrew/Greek. [L3]=Cross-reference. [L4]=Inference -- flag explicitly.
Evaluate ONLY what was argued in this transcript.
THE FRAMEWORK IS NOT A COMBATANT. The framework is the Rules of Engagement and Rules of Interaction -- Isaiah 28:10 method, the L1-L4 layer rules, the burden rule -- and it binds BOTH combatants equally. The debate is about the TOPIC. The DEFENDER holds a chain of interlocking citations on the topic; the CHALLENGER presses an opposing view and settles it only by breaking that chain. A claim by either side that restates resolved ground with its stating verses cited is not an overpress.
This debate was CHAIN-SCOPED (Isaiah 28:10 declared): judge whether the DEFENDER'S CHAIN on the topic survived as a chain -- isolated-link objections that never engage the chain do not break it. The rule cuts both ways: an objection that is itself a floating [L4] (no exhibited layer footing) cannot break a link that exhibits its footing. Name every floating [L4] on either side before weighing it; an inference admitted through the prior layers (Isaiah 28:10) is EVIDENCE, an inference that floats free of the text is not.

COUNTING RULE -- BINDING, read before grading: grade and COUNT only from the two LINK ROUND blocks above, [CR3] (defender) and [DR3] (challenger). The POST-DEBATE ANALYSIS block is CONTEXT ONLY and is NOT evidence of concession -- never take a count from it, and ignore the word "unanswered" wherever it appears there. In this format the challenger link round [DR3] is structurally LAST: no defender round follows it, so the defender being silent after [DR3] is procedural and is NEVER a challenger concession. A premise counts as CONCEDED only if the link round block itself states it as conceded, or exhibits no layer footing for it in that block. UNANSWERED WITNESS: a verse the other side quoted against a link or a listed premise in a round before this link round (against the defender: [DR1] and [DR2]; against the challenger: [CR1], [CR2] and [CR3]) is ANSWERED only when this link round quotes it or names it by reference and shows from the text why it does not overturn the link. Re-citing the link's own footing while passing over the witness pressed against it is not an answer. A premise whose pressed witness goes unanswered is UNEXHIBITED: it counts toward CONCEDED LINKS and the standings. In a debate a non-response is never neutral. A verse on a side's 'cited in' line counts as an unanswered witness only when the judge quotes the sentence of the earlier round that pressed it against that link or a listed premise and names that round; a verse cited there but not pressed against the link does not count. A judge who grades a link BROKEN because the witnesses exclude, deny or establish something must quote the words that state it; where the quoted words, read in their own context and with the other verses the judge quotes, do not state it, the ground is the judge's own [L4] inference, and it counts only when the judge exhibits two lower layers ([L1]-[L3]) for it, as submitted evidence must; otherwise it is no ground for BROKEN, and the link is graded under the remaining rules. UNCHALLENGED: everything a side presents is open to challenge. A link or a witness the other side never challenged in any of its rounds stands as HELD for the side that presented it, and the silent side may not contest it in the verdict. A claim a side restates in its own link round is graded at the layer and with the footing it exhibits there; an earlier [L4] label of the same claim does not survive the restatement.

VERDICT DEFINITIONS -- apply these mechanically, first match wins; not your own scale.
TERMS. DEFENDER LINKS: the links of the DEFENDER'S OWN DECLARED CHAIN as written in [CR1] under its anchored DECLARED CHAIN heading, numbered D1..Dn in that order; n = their number. The LAST declared link, Dn, is the conclusion link. THE ROUND 1 CHAIN GOVERNS. n, the numbering D1..Dn and the keystone Dn come from the DECLARED CHAIN in [CR1] only. A chain the defender declares again in a later round, re-seated, revised or extended, never changes n, the numbering or the keystone: a link added later is not a declared link, and a [CR1] link left out later is still declared. A challenger premise written LINK i: is graded against Di. A challenger premise written LINK none: is JUDGED but is OUTSIDE n and never changes it. The challenger's listed premise count is NOT n and is never substituted for it. CHALLENGER LINKS: the premises listed for the challenger in [DR3], numbered C1..Cm in [DR3] order. WITHDRAWN: a defender link that [CR3] itself states as conceded or withdrawn, wholly or on a stated condition; tag it CONCEDED (stated) or CONCEDED (conditional: <condition>). w = their number. Only the DECLARED links D1..Dn can enter w; a challenger premise written LINK none: is not a defender link and never enters w, however the defender treats it. No link is exempt from w as auxiliary, overreach, non-core or not load-bearing. A link is withdrawn or it is not. Pressure from the challenger's texts alone is not a withdrawal (w); a witness the link round leaves unanswered is graded under UNANSWERED WITNESS and at the COLLISION POINTS. An UNEXHIBITED link counts toward CONCEDED LINKS and the standings, never toward w. BROKEN AT COLLISION: a defender link graded DEFENDER LINK BROKEN on the COLLISION POINTS lines; k = their number.
BROKEN-1: k >= 1 -- a listed defender link refuted from the text.
BROKEN-2: 2w > n, OR the keystone link is WITHDRAWN.
PARTIAL-1: w >= 1.
PARTIAL-2: DEFENSE STANDING is COLLAPSED.
HELD-1: none of the above.
STANDINGS ARE GRADED BY THEIR OWN RULES ONLY. DEFENSE STANDING and CHALLENGE STANDING are each decided by their own definitions below and by nothing else. Any standing is legal with any CLAUSE: the clauses are tried in order and the first match wins, so a COLLAPSED standing under BROKEN-1, BROKEN-2 or PARTIAL-1 is correct and changes no clause. Never choose a standing, a count or a clause to agree with another line, a check or an expected result: when 2c > L the standing is COLLAPSED, whatever the clause.
A SIDE WITH A COUNTED LINK IS NOT SELF-SUPPORTED. If CONCEDED LINKS is 1 or more, DEFENSE STANDING is PROP-DEPENDENT, or COLLAPSED when 2c > L; if CHALLENGER CONCEDED LINKS is 1 or more, CHALLENGE STANDING is PROP-DEPENDENT, or COLLAPSED when 2c > L. SELF-SUPPORTED requires a count of 0 for that side: every premise listed against it exhibited its footing or was VOID.
Line 1: the verdict word first (HELD, PARTIAL or BROKEN), then 2-4 sentences with [L1][L2][L3][L4] tags.
Line 2, exactly: RESULT: DEFENDER WINS | CHALLENGER WINS | UNDERDETERMINED -- one sentence a reader with no background understands. BROKEN gives CHALLENGER WINS. HELD gives DEFENDER WINS only if CHALLENGE STANDING is not SELF-SUPPORTED or at least one collision is graded CHALLENGER LINK BROKEN. Every other case gives UNDERDETERMINED.
Line 3, exactly: CLAUSE: <BROKEN-1 | BROKEN-2 | PARTIAL-1 | PARTIAL-2 | HELD-1> -- n=<n> w=<w> k=<k>

Then SIX MORE mandatory lines, exactly in these forms:
KEYSTONE: D<n> -- WITHDRAWN | BROKEN | STANDS -- name the LAST declared link, Dn, which is the conclusion link, and give its state. WITHDRAWN if it is withdrawn or conceded; BROKEN if it is graded DEFENDER LINK BROKEN at a collision; STANDS otherwise. The number after D MUST equal the n on the CLAUSE line, and the state MUST match that link's own D<n> entry.
DEFENSE STANDING: SELF-SUPPORTED | PROP-DEPENDENT | COLLAPSED -- L=<L> c=<c> -- grade the DEFENDER only, using the STATED/INFERRED tags and the [CR3] LINK ROUND. SELF-SUPPORTED = every listed defender claim is carried by its layer stack -- each [L4] exhibits the [L1]/[L2]/[L3] footing it rests on (at least two prior layers exhibited; a shown chain counts equally). PROP-DEPENDENT = the defense survived only because listed [L4]s were allowed to stand WITHOUT exhibiting their prior-layer footing (floating inference; links conceded/unexhibited in the LINK ROUND). COLLAPSED = by count, mandatory, not discretionary: 2c > L, where L is the number of premises the CHALLENGER listed AGAINST the defender (the UNSUPPORTED-L4 PREMISES list that closes [DR2]) and c is the number of those the defender CONCEDED or left UNEXHIBITED in its LINK ROUND [CR3]. The premises the defender itself lists against the challenger at the end of [CR3] (CHALLENGER'S UNSUPPORTED-L4 PREMISES) are the challenger's burden and NEVER enter the defender's L, c, CONCEDED LINKS or DEFENSE STANDING. FORFEIT: if the challenger listed no premises against the defender (an empty list, or PREMISE COUNT: 0), then L = 0, c = 0, CONCEDED LINKS is 0, DEFENSE STANDING cannot be COLLAPSED, and you add the line LINK ROUND FORFEITED: CHALLENGER -- no premises were listed against the defender. A LINK ROUND answer that neither exhibits the premise's layer footing nor concedes counts as CONCEDED. A tie (2c = L) is NOT COLLAPSED. COLLAPSED is reached by this count only; SELF-SUPPORTED against PROP-DEPENDENT is your judgment as defined above. A listed premise is countable only as charged IF the list carries the opponent's verbatim quote that states it; a premise listed without a verbatim quote, or charged in terms stronger than its quote, is VOID -- it counts for neither side, and a CONCEDED answer to a void premise is NOT a concession: name it VOID in your itemization and exclude it from every count and standing. A link honestly conceded as held inference still counts toward the count but note it as disciplined, not evasive. One sentence of justification.
CONCEDED LINKS: <integer> -- the count of challenger-listed premises the defender CONCEDED or failed to exhibit its layer footing in [CR3]. Itemize the counted links and tag each with the clause that fired: CONCEDED (stated) when the block itself states the surrender; UNEXHIBITED (no footing offered) when the block asserted the link and offered no layer footing at all. A link whose block exhibits at least two prior layers of footing and shows the chain from them to the link is SUPPORTED and is not counted, even when no cited verse states the link's conclusion in words: an [L4] is an inference by definition, and demanding a verse that states it is the retired stating-verse test, which is never applied. When the count is lower than the number of links, name which links are counted and which are spared. Both tags count toward the single integer; keep the tags distinct in the itemization and do not report more than one number.
CHALLENGE STANDING: SELF-SUPPORTED | PROP-DEPENDENT | COLLAPSED -- L=<L> c=<c> -- grade the CHALLENGER only, using the challenger's STATED/INFERRED tags and the [DR3] LINK ROUND. SELF-SUPPORTED = every listed objection is carried by its layer stack -- each [L4] exhibits the [L1]/[L2]/[L3] footing it rests on. PROP-DEPENDENT = the challenge survived only because listed [L4]s were allowed to stand without exhibiting their prior-layer footing (floating inference; links conceded/unexhibited in [DR3]). COLLAPSED = by count, mandatory, not discretionary: 2c > L, where L is the number of premises the DEFENDER listed AGAINST the challenger (the CHALLENGER'S UNSUPPORTED-L4 PREMISES list that closes [CR3]) and c is the number of those the challenger CONCEDED or left UNEXHIBITED in its LINK ROUND [DR3]. The premises the challenger listed against the defender (the list that closes [DR2]) NEVER enter the challenger's L, c, CHALLENGER CONCEDED LINKS or CHALLENGE STANDING. FORFEIT: if the defender listed no premises against the challenger (an empty list), then L = 0, c = 0, CHALLENGER CONCEDED LINKS is 0, CHALLENGE STANDING cannot be COLLAPSED, and you add the line LINK ROUND FORFEITED: DEFENDER -- no premises were listed against the challenger. A LINK ROUND answer that neither exhibits the premise's layer footing nor concedes counts as CONCEDED. A tie (2c = L) is NOT COLLAPSED. COLLAPSED is reached by this count only; SELF-SUPPORTED against PROP-DEPENDENT is your judgment as defined above. A void premise (no verbatim quote, or charged beyond its quote) counts for neither side. A link honestly conceded as held inference still counts toward the count but note it as disciplined, not evasive. One sentence of justification.
CHALLENGER CONCEDED LINKS: <integer> -- the count of defender-listed premises the challenger CONCEDED or failed to exhibit its layer footing in [DR3]. Itemize the counted links and tag each with the clause that fired: CONCEDED (stated) when the block itself states the surrender; UNEXHIBITED (no footing offered) when the block asserted the link and offered no layer footing at all. A link whose block exhibits at least two prior layers of footing and shows the chain from them to the link is SUPPORTED and is not counted, even when no cited verse states the link's conclusion in words: an [L4] is an inference by definition, and demanding a verse that states it is the retired stating-verse test, which is never applied. When the count is lower than the number of links, name which links are counted and which are spared. Both tags count toward the single integer; keep the tags distinct in the itemization and do not report more than one number.
COLLISION POINTS: the label on its own line, then one entry per line covering every defender link D1..Dn, none omitted. A link counted in w: D<i>: WITHDRAWN. A link that a challenger link contradicts about the same text: D<i> vs C<j> (<verse>): DEFENDER LINK BROKEN | CHALLENGER LINK BROKEN | UNDERDETERMINED -- one clause of why; one such line per contradicting challenger link, and DEFENDER LINK BROKEN at most once per defender link. Any other link: D<i>: STANDS. CHAIN SCOPE (Isaiah 28:10): no single verse is required to state a link or its contrary. The witnesses a side exhibits, read together line upon line, are the text's own statement, and "no single verse states it" is never a ground for STANDS or UNDERDETERMINED. Grade a link BROKEN when the other side exhibited [L1]/[L2]/[L3] witnesses that together contradict it and the broken side's link round left any of those witnesses UNANSWERED (COUNTING RULE). Grade UNDERDETERMINED only when the link round answered every such witness and the witnesses, read together, still leave the point open. k is the number of lines graded DEFENDER LINK BROKEN. A side with any link graded BROKEN here cannot be SELF-SUPPORTED.

DEBATE RULE VIOLATION (Isaiah 28:10: "here a little, [and] there a little"). The rule on a BROKEN ground binds you as it binds the debaters. On every COLLISION POINTS line you grade DEFENDER LINK BROKEN or CHALLENGER LINK BROKEN, exhibit on that line itself at least two lower layers of DIFFERENT kinds, each tagged: [L1] with the KJV words quoted; [L2] with the Hebrew or Greek word in its own script, with its transliteration marks, or with its Strong's number; [L3] with the cross-reference named. Two quotations at one layer are one layer. A BROKEN line without them is a DEBATE RULE VIOLATION. The system checks every BROKEN line after you answer; a violating line is returned to you to cure, by exhibiting the two layers, or to answer in writing why it stands. An answer that does not exhibit them leaves the violation standing, and your judgment is then not counted. The same holds when your CONCEDED LINKS or CHALLENGER CONCEDED LINKS count is 0 while you grade a link of that side BROKEN for a witness left unanswered. Both sides alike.

Named inside the text served to the CHALLENGER: (none). Conclusions are #N, sights are S-N -- separate namespaces. A label in a side's named list is not a citation error: it carries only what the served sentence naming it says. Any conclusion or sight label a side cites that is in neither of that side's own lists was not served to it this run: treat it as unverified ground, do not let it carry weight, and name it in your justification as a citation error.

CITATION CHECK AGAINST THE KJV -- computed by the system from the KJV text, not by a model: 204 quotations given with a reference; 190 match the verse cited; 0 do not; 14 too short to check.

PRESSED-WITNESS LEDGER -- computed by the system from the transcript text, not by a model, by one rule for both sides. SCOPE: the verses carried by each numbered premise of the list that closes [DR2] (pressed against the defender) and of the CHALLENGER'S UNSUPPORTED-L4 PREMISES list that closes [CR3] (pressed against the challenger), and whether the answering link round names each verse anywhere. The line beginning "cited in" under each side lists every verse the opposing side cited in the rounds before that link round that the link round does not name; which of those verses were quoted against a link or a listed premise is the judge's to decide. OUT-OF-ROUND CONCESSIONS -- after the COLLISION POINTS lines, add a section titled exactly OUT-OF-ROUND CONCESSIONS, the title on its own line. List one line for each concession either side made OUTSIDE the two link rounds -- that is, ground granted in [CR1], [DR1], [CR2] or [DR2], not in [CR3] or [DR3]. Form, one per line: SIDE | ROUND | the verbatim sentence in which the ground was granted, where SIDE is DEFENDER or CHALLENGER. If there are none, write exactly: NONE. BINDING: these are recorded separately and DO NOT ENTER CONCEDED LINKS, CHALLENGER CONCEDED LINKS, DEFENSE STANDING, CHALLENGE STANDING, the CLAUSE line or the top-line verdict. The counting rule above is unchanged and still governs those lines: a premise counts as CONCEDED only from the link round block itself. Never sum an out-of-round concession with a link-round count.

DEFENDER CHAIN MARK: <FOOTED|STRAINED|SLACK> -- <one clause naming the load-bearing link that decides the mark>.
CHALLENGER CHAIN MARK: <FOOTED|STRAINED|SLACK> -- <one clause naming the load-bearing link that decides the mark>.
These two lines come last, after OUT-OF-ROUND CONCESSIONS, the defender's first. One definition marks each side's own constructed chain, the defender's declared chain (D1 to the conclusion link) and the challenger's own alternative reading: FOOTED = every load-bearing link of the side's chain is footed on an exhibited L1-L3 layer; its reading stands as a built structure. STRAINED = the chain is built but one or more load-bearing links float, asserted at L4 without exhibited footing; it stands only under charity. SLACK = the side advanced no coherent chain of its own: objections without a constructed reading, or a construction that never cohered. Each mark describes ONLY how well that side built its own chain. It is descriptive, not a verdict. It MUST NOT change DEFENSE STANDING, CHALLENGE STANDING, CONCEDED LINKS, CHALLENGER CONCEDED LINKS, the CLAUSE line, KEYSTONE or the top-line verdict. A FOOTED chain is fully compatible with its side losing the debate; a SLACK chain is fully compatible with its side winning it.

If the transcript genuinely will not settle it, choose the tag the rulebook gives on the evidence that is there and say plainly in reasoning what is unresolved. A hung panel is a legitimate outcome of three honest judges; it is not your job to manufacture agreement.
